## Document 015: 2026-10-01_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］対日直接投資拡大で国内供給力の強化へ_f76b5ad3

みずほ経済 EXPRESS

対日直接投資拡大で国内供給力の強化へ 
～戦略 17 分野でみた外資誘致の現状と課題～

調査部  エコノミスト 阿部大樹 
080-3709-6077 taiki.abe@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 高まる国内投資の機運。 国内供給力強化に向けては対内直接投資の取り込みも重要に 
政府は2026年7月、 日本成長戦略会議において、 国内供給力の強化に向けた官民投資ロードマップを
決定した。とりわけ経済安全保障上の重要性や成長期待が高い17の戦略分野においては、2040年度ま
でに累計370兆円超の官民投資が想定されている（図表1）。日本政策投資銀行「2026年度設備投資計
画調査」では、大企業の約58％、中堅企業の約43％が戦略分野における国内投資を強化すると答えて
おり、多くの企業が政府の方針を前向きに受け止めていることがうかがえる。 
国内投資促進の主軸を担うのは政府や国内民間企業だが、国内供給力を強化していくうえでは、外
国資本の取り込み、すなわち対内直接投資の拡大を促すことも重要だ。対内直接投資とは、外国企業
が日本企業への出資や融資を通じて、日本国内の企業経営・事業に継続的に関わることを目的とした
投資のことをいう。政府も対日直接投資推進会議で、戦略分野ごとに有望な外国企業を発掘し誘致す
る方針を掲げている。 
対内直接投資は、 投資形態の違いから四つに分類できる （図表2） 。 このうち、 「合併・買収」 や 「企
図表1 戦略17分野 図表2 投資形態別分類

が、具体的な想定がわからない分については除外

### Page 2

2

業・財務再編」は国内供給力の強化につながらない可能性もあるが、「グリーンフィールド投資」と
「能力増強投資」は工場・生産ラインなど生産資本の増加をともない、供給網のレジリエンス向上や
国内の供給力強化につながりやすい形態だ。本稿では、これら二つの形態を合わせて「国内供給力強
化投資」と呼ぶこととする。以下では「国内供給力強化投資」に焦点を絞り、戦略分野ごとに直近5年
間の投資流入状況を確認したうえで、対内直接投資の今後の増加余地と誘致のあり方を検討する。 
■ AI・データセンター関連投資等のグローバルな機運が国内供給力強化投資をけん引  
日本の対内直接投資は、2010年代半ば以降、拡大傾向にある。国際収支統計で集計される対内直接
投資の動向を示した図表3をみると、 その力強い伸びが顕著に見て取れる。GDP比でも1.0％ 程 度（2025
年）まで増加しており、外国企業の国内投資志向が高まりつつある証左と言えよう。 
先行きは、さらなる対内直接投資の拡大が期待できそうだ。予定段階の個別投資案件を集計してい
るfDi Marketsのデータで国内供給力強化投資の動向を確認すると、2020年代に入って投資金額が大幅
に拡大していることがわかる （図表4）。同データでみられる投資拡大の動きは、クロスボーダー金融
取引の実績を計上している国際収支統計でも今後顕在化してくるとみられる。 
さらに、個別投資案件のデータを確認することで、国際収支統計では把握できなかった対内直接投
資の性質・内容を詳細に分析することが可能である。戦略分野別に国内供給力強化投資をみると、AI・
半導体分野や、データセンター関連が含まれるデジタル・サイバーセキュリティ分野で投資金額が大
きく、 かつ大幅な伸びが確認できる （図表5） 。 世界的なAIブームを受けたデータセンター関連投資の
活発化が背景とみられる。半導体製造設備やデータセンター関連の投資には巨額の資本が必要になる
ため、投資金額を件数で割った一件あたりの投資額が大規模化しやすく、大型投資案件が投資額全体
を押し上げていることがうかがえる。 
次に投資金額が大きかったのは資源・エネルギー安全保障・GX分野だ。具体的には、発電所などの
電力供給設備への投資案件が押し上げている。太陽光発電・洋上風力発電などの再生可能エネルギー
市場で海外企業の参入が増えた結果とみられ、脱炭素化を志向するグローバルな機運が追い風になっ

ていると言える。加えて、電力需要の大きいデータセンターの立地を最適化するため、電力源と通信
インフラを一体的に整備する「ワットビット連携」が推進される中で、電力供給設備への投資需要が
高まっていることも当該分野の投資押上げに寄与しているとみられる。 
これらの分野では引き続きグローバルトレンドに沿った投資流入増が見込めそうである一方、造船
分野や防衛産業分野、量子分野、海洋分野の投資額は、増加しているものの依然として小規模だ。例
えば、量子分野は研究開発～実証段階の技術が中心の新領域であり、分野の将来的な成長期待は大き
い。現時点では収益化まで距離があることから、投資額の小ささ自体が問題とは言えないものの、米
国等と比べると日本の産業基盤は相対的に小さく、 スタートアップ企業の集積でも遅れを取っている。
さらに投資を呼び込むうえでは、こうした遅れを埋める手立てを講じることが必要不可欠であろう。 
また、造船分野では、2020年代に入ってから中国の存在感が一段と高まっている。日本の造船会社
による船舶の新規受注量は2000年代半ばから減少しており、世界の船舶建造量に占める日本のシェア
も低下傾向だ。背景には、中国と比べた事業所規模の小ささが指摘されており、生産コストなどの面
で水をあけられている。 
■ 幅広い分野での外国資本取り込みに向けて供給面 ・ 制度面のボトルネック軽減が重要 
戦略分野全体でみれば投資流入の堅調さが確認できた一方で、17の戦略分野それぞれにおいて投資
流入増を実現していく1ためには、 投資の門戸を開くだけでは不十分だ。 造船分野や量子分野に関して
指摘したような課題の解決に向けては、ボトルネックを軽減する地道な取り組みが必要であろう。ま
た、足元で急速に拡大しているデータセンター投資や再エネ関連投資についても、今後は成長の維持
が課題となる。 
外国資本取り込みにおける具体的なボトルネックとして、人材不足等の供給制約や複雑な制度、言
語障壁が挙げられる。日本国内の外資系企業を対象にした、日本貿易振興機構による「2025年度外資
系企業ビジネス実態調査」 では、 直近1～2年の日本のビジネス環境の課題について、 多くの企業が 「高
度人材等の確保」 と 「一般人材の確保」 を回答しているほか、 直近では 「オフィス・事業用地の確保」
図表5 戦略17分野別にみた国内供給力強化投資額の推移

ては除外。各社の個別投資プロジェクトの情報及び、日本成長戦略会議資料を基に、みずほ総合研究所調査部が分類

### Page 4

4

を回答する企業の割合も高まっている。こうした人材・立地面等の供給制約は、外資の取り込みを進
めるうえで課題となる。 
また、制度面について、日本貿易振興機構による「ジェトロ対日投資報告2018」では、外国企業が
日本でビジネス展開するうえでの阻害要因として「行政手続きの複雑さ」や「許認可制度の厳しさ」
等が挙げられ、外国企業にとって日本のビジネス環境への適応が容易ではないことがうかがえる。な
お、 同調査では 「外国語によるコミュニケーションの難しさ」 が2位にランクするなど、 言語障壁も外
国企業の参入を阻む一因だ。 
供給面・制度面のボトルネック解消に向けた対応として、人材不足については、大学等の研究・教
育機関との連携強化を通じた国内人材の育成・確保や、高度外国人材の受入強化による対応が考えら
れる。また、用地不足に対しては、産業用地の確保や空き家・空き地の有効活用を通じて、外国企業
が拠点を設立しやすい環境を整備することが重要だろう。 
制度面の対応としては、まず、手続きの一本化が挙げられる。現状、外国企業等の投資申請に際し
ては担当省庁・公的機関と個別にやり取りするケースがあり、外国企業の手続き負担は小さいとは言
えない。 この点、OECD（2026） は外国企業 ・ 投資家と関係省庁等との調整を担う 「ワンストップ窓口」
の機能強化を提言している。 言語障壁については近年通訳や英訳による手続き支援が進みつつあるが、
主要な政策等については英語アナウンスを適時提供するなど、言語障壁軽減に向けたさらなる取り組
みが求められる。

［参考文献］ 
OECD (2022), FDI Qualities Policy Toolkit, OECD Publishing 
OECD (2025), OECD Benchmark Definition of Foreign Direct Investment,  5th edition, OECD 
Publishing 
OECD (2026), OECD Economic Surveys: Japan 2026, OECD Publishing

5

1 コンテンツ産業のような日本企業に競争力がある分野では、外国資本の取り込みによる事業拡大も期待される一方、独自技術
や知的財産の流出リスクには留意し、慎重に議論を進める必要がある。

UMC (2303.TW) From To
Price Target NT$138.00 NT$188.00
TSMC (2330.TW) From To
Price Target NT$2,988.00 NT$3,088.00
We think the capex budget might gradually shift to AI compute
and AI networking in 2028, as memory usage needs to be
optimized. We see positives for TSMC and UMC.
TSMC 3Q26 earnings preview: We expect the company to raise full-year revenue
guidance to the low-to-mid-40% range (vs.  above 40%) Y/Y, implying 4Q26 revenue

will rise 10%-15% Q/Q amid very strong 3nm demand. 3Q26 gross margin should
reach the high end of guidance of 67.5% However, should Intel focus on internal
capacity expansion, we think TSMC would maintain 2nm capacity expansion at the
same pace and its capex at US$54bn for 2026. For 2027, we see TSMC’s capex
reaching US$75bn to US$80bn.
In terms of 2027 wafer price hikes, we see leading edge (7nm and below) up 5%-10%
and mature nodes up 0%-3%; hence, gross margin could approach 70% in 2027. As a

result, we raise  our TSMC price target  by 3% to NT$3,088.
Key topics for upcoming TSMC 3Q26 earnings call on October 15:
1. Would TSMC consider working with TereFab  (Taiwan News, Oct 3 2026) and
building new fab sites in Texas (Bloomberg, Oct 1 2026)? Our view is that expansion
in Texas could be necessary, considering customer demand and limited space at the
Arizona fab in five years.
2. If Intel were to increase capex, would that reduce outsourcing at TSMC? Our

recent industry checks suggest some trimming of Intel’s 3nm and 2nm; how might
TSMC reallocate its resources?
3. How would TSMC address large reticle size chip demand? Our checks suggest
MediaTek handled TPU v10 IceFish reticle size initially at 14x; could TSMC’s CoWoS
or CoPoS be able to support such packaging?
We raise  our UMC price target by 36% to NT$188 on AI networking (SiPh)
business upside: Some investors remain on the sidelines amid ongoing smartphone

and PC demand weakness. We have seen some order cuts in PC semis recently, as
some consumer analog IC customers (e.g., Silergy) have not been able to pass
through UMC’s wafer price hike. Therefore, some investors do not expect any UMC’s
price hike in 2027. However, two growth drivers could help UMC raise wafer price
for 8-inch in 2027: 1) Samsung DDI outsourcing, as we noted in our earlier  Foundry
Floorplan, and 2) growing SiPh PIC demand, in particular after UMC introduced its

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
October 5, 2026 08:00 PM GMT

TSMC's 2026–28 foundry floorplan broadly aligns with our
bottom-up demand analysis
Our industry checks suggest ASML allocates capacity based largely on historical demand
and long-term partnerships, indicating that TSMC remains the largest customer for EUV
shipments. If so, TSMC should retain strong pricing power and could increase leading-edge
wafer prices by 5%–10% in 2027, reflecting both the value it delivers to customers and the
potential for margin expansion.

TSMC 2nm and 3nm capacity growth remains robust; 5nm
set to decline
Consistent with our demand forecast, TSMC indicated very strong capacity requirements
for both 2nm and 3nm starting in 2026, implying a 70% capacity CAGR for N2 from 2026

to 2028. TSMC also suggested that 5nm capacity will begin to decline from 2027, in line
with our forecast that 5nm demand will drop as Blackwell transitions to Rubin on 3nm.
Separately, advanced packaging capacity expansion should remain in focus for TSMC in
2027. This includes, but is not limited to, CoWoS and SoIC, for which we now expect
capacity to reach 200kwpm and 40kwpm, respectively, by 2027.

How might TSMC address Intel's competition in both wafer
foundry and advanced packaging?
Intel EMIB-T remains competitive in 2029; how TSMC will
address?
With demand in AI continuing to boom, we expect TSMC and its OSAT partners to
expedite capacity expansion plans for CoWoS. We see TSMC's CoWoS capacity building
further to 260kwpm in 2028, from 130/200kwpm in 2026/2027, with 2028's expansion
mainly focused in Arizona's AP9/10 and potentially in AP7. TSMC is now conducting trial

production for CoPoS in Taoyuan. If the progress goes well, we would expect TSMC to
start building its CoPoS capacity, with location also likely to be in AP9/10 or AP7P3.
We expect the non-TSMC camp to expand CoWoS capacity to 110kwpm by 2028 year-end,
from 80kwpm by end-2027. For ASE/SPIL, we expect FoCoS+CoWoS capacity to rise from
30kwpm at 2027-end to 50-60kwpm at 2028-end, with expansion focused on CoWoS-L
and CoWoS-R. For Amkor, we expect CoWoS capacity to increase from 30kwpm at 2027-

end to 55-60kwpm at 2028-end, also focused on CoWoS-L and CoWoS-R.

What about  Intel EMIB capacity build?
For Intel, we see current average EMIB-M capacity of 110kwpm falling slightly to 95kwpm
in 2027 as it converts some toward EMIB-T. We see the major customers of EMIB-M being
Trainium3, upcoming Trainium 4 and its in-house server CPU. Average EMIB-T capacity is

around 5kwpm in 2026, which we expect to further scale to 15-20kwpm in 2027 and 40-
45kwpm in 2028. All of the capacity mentioned above is converted for  12-inch wafer
equivalent, which we believe would be much more easier to communicate with compared
to TSMC CoWoS capacity.
Although some small projects are ongoing, we still see MediaTek/Google Humufish
consuming most of the capacity during 2027-28. We maintain our view that Intel EMIB-T

Indeed, we have seen some TSMC customers surveying (testing vehicles) Intel EMIB-T due
to the design for larger chip size, lower cost, and intention to seek a second source
because TSMC's CoWoS capacity is still relatively tight. However, we believe TSMC could
still try to expand its packaging size by leveraging different technologies to meet
customers' needs. We think it remains concerned about whether it is going to open its
front-end computing die production/know-how to its competitor, Intel, for back-end

packaging.
Our prediction of the trend after 2028 –  TSMC might:
1. Pull in CoPoS (panel-level process) with both 300mm and 500mm square panel
size
2. Introduce 3.5D packaging with SoIC for vertical chip stacking (Meta MTIA is
surveying)
3. Adopt glass core substrate to solve warpage for >9.7x reticle-size chips

Pros and cons of TSMC's CoWoS, Intel's EMIB, TSMC's
CoPoS, and SoIC
TSMC’s CoWoS, Intel’s EMIB, TSMC’s CoPoS, and TSMC’s SoIC represent different points
along the advanced packaging spectrum, each optimizing for a different balance of
bandwidth, cost, scalability and integration complexity. We think future design of the
chips is likely to combine these approaches—for example, using CoWoS for lateral

integration and SoIC for vertical stacking—to achieve optimal system-level performance.
Exhibit 12: Comparison between TSMC's CoWoS, CoPoS, SoIC, and Intel's EMIB-T

In summary,

### Page 9

• CoWoS excels in high-bandwidth 2.5D integration but is costly and capacity-limited
• EMIB offers a more cost-effective and modular alternative with some performance
trade-offs
• CoPoS targets large-scale, cost-efficient integration but is still maturing
• SoIC delivers the highest performance through 3D stacking at the expense of
complexity and thermal constraints.

### Page 10

M
Idea
10
UMC is well positioned for Silicon Photonic IC (PIC) foundry
In our previous Foundry Floorplan, we presented a constructive view on UMC given its AI-
related revenue. Management indicated AI revenue of US$300mn this year (3-5% of
revenue), potentially more than tripling to US$1bn in three years (2029).
UMC has a  capacity expansion plan to capture AI demand, including a clean room for
advanced packaging for AI chips, such as wafer-on-wafer and memory-on-memory stacking.

Some investors remain on the sidelines amid ongoing smartphone and PC demand
weakness, and we have seen some order cuts of PC semis recently. Some think a UMC
price hike in 2027 wouldn’t go through, but we see two growth drivers helping it
successfully  hike wafer price for 8-inch in 2027: 1) Samsung DDI outsourcing, and 2)
growing SiPh PIC demand, in particular after UMC introduced its 400Gb per lane solution
(PIC + TFLN) solution.
How UMC is differentiated in PIC foundry

According to UMC, InP and TFLN are the two most promising solutions, as they are much
more mature than the other contenders. InP is facing severe shortage, adding to TFLN’s
momentum.
UMC Group’s TFLN program started in 2021 with 6” in mass production and 8” in
sampling. TFLN's   strengths include low loss, high bandwidth (> 110 GHz), good thermal
stability, and strong linearity.
Integrating TFLN with PIC is a challenge as well as an opportunity. Advanced packaging

schemes and micro transfer printing are both seen as promising candidates. UMC is
developing the integration technology, leveraging its expertise in PIC, TFLN, and advanced
packaging.
Adoption of higher-performance modulators (TFLN/InP) and improvement in photo diodes
are key factors affecting deployment of plain-vanilla (non-WDM, IM/DD) DCIs with 400
Gbps/channel performance and beyond, which is very close to TSMC's mainstream PIC
technology Micro Ring.

Exhibit 15: UMC differentiates in TFLN process offering for PIC
Source: UMC

About Foundry Floorplan
In 2011,  we first published this product (Asia/Pacific Foundry: The Foundry Floorplan) to
provide our analysis of foundry industry dynamics. We have resumed this practice as
global foundry competition remains active. Foundry supply is also a critical resource for
future AI chips, whether in the global market or China's local market.
Below are prior reports from this series:
• The Foundry Floorplan: All boats riding on the same tide – mature node up-cycle

begins (19 May 2026)
• The Foundry Floorplan: TSMC preview update; CoWoS vs. EMIB competition (14
Apr 2026)
• The Foundry Floorplan: Raise TSMC's 2027e and 2028e Capex (9 Mar 2026)
• The Foundry Floorplan: Update on TSMC's 2027 capex; interposer outsourcing to
Vanguard? (13 Jan 2026)
• Asia/Pacific Foundry: The Foundry Floorplan, April 2011

TSMC: Estimate and price target revisions
Our earnings forecasts increase 4% for 2026,  5% for  2027 and 3% for 2028: We raise
our 2026 revenue forecast and modestly increase our margin assumptions to reflect
stronger-than-expected 3Q26 performance, driven by robust AI demand, including
sustained Blackwell shipments. We raise our 2027–28 revenue assumptions by 3%–5%,
reflecting the increase in strong leading-edge demand as well as the wafer price hike.

Our PT increases to NT$3,088 from   NT$2,988, driven primarily by our estimate

revisions. Our PT implies a 20x P/E multiple on our new 2027 EPS forecast. TSMC
currently trades at 17x that estimate, close to its average NTM P/E of 16.5x since 2018. We
find the stock attractive at current levels.
We continue to derive our 12-month price target (base-case scenario) using a residual
income valuation framework. Our key assumptions are unchanged, including a cost of
equity of 9.2% (beta of 1.2, equity risk premium of 6.0%, and risk-free rate of 2.0%), an

intermediate growth rate of 10.5%, and a terminal growth rate of 4%.
Our bull and bear case scenario values increase to NT$3,720 (from NT$3,600) and NT
$1,700 (from NT$1,645), respectively.

UMC: Estimate and price target revisions
We raise our 2026, 2027, and 2028 earnings estimates by 56%, 25%, and 31%,
respectively: Our 2026e change is due mostly to strong 2Q26 non-op gains. We increase
2027/28e revenue and EPS due to new CPO related business contribution from 12-inch
SiPh PIC  as well as 8-inch TFLN. Our 2028e increase also includes DDI outsource from
Samsung foundry.

We raise our price target to NT$188 from NT$138: We continue to derive our price
target (base case scenario value) from a residual income model, as we believe it best
represents the value of the company. Besides our estimate increases, we raise our
intermediate growth rate from 12.5% to 13.5% due to structural changes in the industry's
supply/demand dynamics. Other key assumptions are unchanged, including a cost of

equity of 9.2% (beta of 1.2, equity risk premium of 6%, and risk-free rate of 2%),  and
terminal growth rate of 3%.
Our bull case scenario value increases from NT$200 to NT$270, and our bear case value
rises from NT$70 to  NT$95, implying 7.5x and 2.6x 2027e BVPS, respectively.

Consensus Price Target Distribution NT$38.30 NT$258.00
Source: Reﬁnitiv, Morgan Stanley Research
MS Rating
▪ We are OW as we see structural changes
in the mature node industry's
supply/demand dynamics.
▪ Its foundry quality is comparable to
TSMC's in mature nodes. Along with

specialty technologies, this could help offset
part of the margin downside.
▪ Non-AI market recovery remains uncertain
in 2027, yet UMC should still beneﬁt from
strong AI-related demand spillover from its
foundry peers.
▪ 12nm FinFET development is strategic. It
should be one of UMC's long-term
strengths, notably when Chinese foundries
cannot effectively expand FinFET capacity.
▪ The stock now trades at 4.1x 2027e BVPS.
That is well above its historical average

since 2009, but our price target implies 5.1x.

NT$270.00
7.5x 2027e BVPS
Stabilized semi demand; better-than-
expected 8-inch and trailing-edge 12-inch
demand: 1) Global semi sales grow >15% Y/Y

in 2026, and ex-TSMC foundry industry
grows >10% Y/Y; 2) UMC gains share in both
8- and 12-inch wafers thanks to technology
differentiation; 3) UMC receives more
outsourcing from customers, which helps ﬁll
22/28nm capacity and lifts blended wafer
ASP; 4) traction increases for UMC's
12nm/14nm FinFET technologies.
NT$188.00
5.2x 2027e BVPS
Healthy semi growth; improving demand for
8-inch and trailing-edge 12-inch: 1) Global
semi sales grow ~15% in 2026 and ex-TSMC

foundry industry grows 3-5% Y/Y; 2) 8-inch
utilization rate is above 75% with steady 12-
inch demand amid semi inventory digestion;
3) UMC hikes wafer prices slightly in
response to strong AI-related demand.
NT$95.00
2.6x 2027e BVPS
Signiﬁcant semi industry decline; 8-inch and
trailing-edge 12-inch face weakening
demand: 1) Global semi sales grow only <5%
in 2026; 2) display semi and power semi
demand deteriorates further; 3) signiﬁcant
wafer pricing erosion given low fab

utilization and mature node peers'
competition; 4) depreciation ramp-up of
more than 30% Y/Y in 2026 without
sufﬁcient wafer order inﬂow to sustain
UMC's gross margin.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Update
Accton Technology Corporation  | Asia Pacific
September Sales -1% MoM/
+66% YoY
In this report, we focus on Accton's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for share price movement.
Details:
• September sales were NT$40,399mn (-1% MoM/+66% YoY).
• 3Q26 revenue was NT$120,744mn (+26% QoQ/+66% YoY), in line with MSe
of NT$120,883mn (+27% QoQ/+66% YoY)  and consensus of NT$117.390mn
(+23% QoQ/+61% YoY) by 3%.
Our view:
• We believe the consignment arrangement of DRAM, PCB, etc., from August

might have capped the upside on revenue growth, but it should be a
favorable factor for gross margin, emerging from August/September and
likely extending into 4Q26.
• Driven by the network switch business and its major customer's AI
accelerator module ramp, we expect revenue to continue growing
sequentially in 4Q26, with the momentum in 2027 strengthened by potential
new customers and new products.
• Stay OW with a PT of NT$3,500, based on 30x 2027e P/E.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Accton Technology Corporation (2345.TW)
Base case, P/E, which we believe better captures the company's earnings growth momentum.
We assign a target P/E of 30x to our 2027 EPS estimate, which we view as justified by an
operating profit CAGR of 48% for 2025-28, based on our estimates. This implies a PEG of
around 0.6, vs. its average of 0.7 over 2019-25, when the stock was trading at a P/E of 22x for
an operating profit CAGR of 32%.

Risks to Upside
n Stronger data center capex
n Faster network switch spec migration
n InfiniBand losing share in AI networks
n Stronger custom AI accelerator growth
n Stronger ODM direct business model adoption
Risks to Downside
n Weaker data center capex
n Slower network switch spec migration
n InfiniBand taking more share in AI networks
n Softer custom AI accelerator growth
n Softer ODM direct business model adoption

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

## Document 101: 9月外贸数据点评：出口超预期，结构改善加快

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月外贸数据点评：出口超预期，结构改善加快.pdf
Report source: 联储证券
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 1

9 月外贸数据点评：出口超预期，结构改善加快

[Table_Author] 
 魏争 分析师  沈夏宜 分析师

Email:weizheng@lczq.com Email:shenxiayi@lczq.com  
证书:S1320524100001 证书:S1320523020004

报告要点： 
出口明显超预期，增长的覆盖范围进一步扩大 。9 月，以美元计价，出口
同比增长 36.3%，较 8 月提高 11.3 个百分点，远高于万得一致预期
25.7%；环比增长 11.4%，也明显强于往年同期。出口超预期主要来自两
方面支撑，一方面，全球制造业景气继续改善，AI 产业链需求保持强劲。
9 月全球制造业 PMI 由 8 月的 52.3 升至 53.0，继续创近 4 年来新高，另
一方面，传统产业出口延续修复趋势，增长的覆盖范围进一步扩大，与 AI
产业链共同支撑出口增长。 
分产品看，机电产品仍是主要动力，传统商品修复明显加快 。一是，AI 产
业链维持高景气，优势制造业继续提供核心支撑。9 月，集成电路、自动
数据处理设备出口分别增长 136.0%和 92.9%，合计拉动整体出口增长
13.0 个百分点；汽车、家用电器出口分别增长 62.0%和 31.7%。机电产
品整体增长 43.7%，拉动出口 27.6 个百分点，贡献约四分之三的出口增
量。二是，传统商品普遍加快，出口修复的覆盖范围扩大。箱包、纺织
品、服装、鞋靴、家具和玩具出口分别增长 22.5%、19.0%、23.8%、
20.2%、22.0%和 40.8%，均高于 8 月。六类商品合计增长 23.0%，拉动
整体出口 2.6 个百分点，较 8 月增加约 1.4 个百分点。传统商品对出口的
支撑有所增强，但机电产品仍主导本轮增长。我们分析传统商品出口增速
改善有两方面原因，一方面，低基数放大了部分商品的同比修复。2025
年 9 月箱包、服装、鞋靴和玩具出口分别下降 12.3%、8.0%、13.3%和
28.0%。其中，本月玩具虽增长 40.8%，较 2024 年同期的两年复合增速
仅约 0.7%，或指向本月高增主要是对前期下滑的修复性回补。另一方
面，节日备货及交付前移可能影响月度出货。部分义乌圣诞用品商户今年
接单情况好于去年1；受生产、海运均需预留周期等因素影响，部分节日
商品提前安排交付，可能对近期玩具、箱包等消费品出口形成阶段性支
撑。 
分地区看，主要市场普遍改善，东盟和欧盟对出口加速的贡献尤为突出 。
一是，亚洲市场维持强劲增长，东盟贡献明显扩大。9 月，对东盟出口增
长 52.3%，较 8 月提高 22.1 个百分点，创 2022 年以来最高增速，其中，
对越南出口增长 53.3%；对中国香港、韩国分别增长 51.0%和 68.2%。东
盟、中国香港和韩国合计拉动整体出口 16.5 个百分点，较 8 月增加 3.9
个百分点，其中东盟拉动由 5.4 个百分点升至 8.5 个百分点。中国香港同
比增速虽回落，出口金额环比仍增长 18.1%。二是，对欧洲及其他主要市
相关报告  
9 月 FOMC：通胀回落仍不足，政策路径
整体偏鹰，AI 投资成为新的宏观变量 
2026.10.12 
美国 8 月 CPI：核心通胀超预期反弹，市
场重新定价利率路径 
2026.09.16 
8 月金融数据点评：财政与准财政加力有
望结束社融增速单边下行 
2026.09.16

### Page 2

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 2 
场出口明显改善。9 月，对欧盟出口增长 25.3%，较 8 月提高 18.6 个百
分点，拉动由 1.1 个百分点升至 3.7 个百分点；对德国、俄罗斯和加拿大
分别增长 31.4%、51.1%和 31.8%。三是，对美国出口继续修复。9 月，
对美出口增长 39.6%，较 8 月提高 5.2 个百分点，拉动整体出口 4.1 个百
分点；环比增长 12.7%，较上月明显加快。低基数仍是对美出口修复的重
要支撑。去年同期对美出口下降 27.0%，本月出口金额较 2024 年同期仅
高 1.8%，趋势性恢复仍需观察。 
从量价看，数量贡献增强、量价结构边际改善，整体增长仍保留较强的价
格驱动特征。一方面，传统消费品及部分装备产品的数量改善更为明显。
9 月，箱包和鞋靴出口数量分别增长约 11.5%和 10.1%，出口均价分别上
涨 9.9%和 9.2%，金额增长开始获得数量端支撑。家用电器、汽车和铝材
出口数量分别增长 25.4%、55.5%和 26.1%出口，均价分别上涨 5.0%、
4.2%和 16.3%，数量贡献较为突出。另一方面，电子产品仍具有较强的
均价驱动特征。集成电路出口数量仅增长约 1.1%，均价上涨 133.5%；手
机数量下降 12.6%，均价上涨 30.6%，价格仍是金额增长的主要支撑。在
跟踪的 11 类商品中，8 类呈现量价齐升，但船舶、液晶显示模组均价有
所下降，钢材数量基本持平。 
进口维持高增，机电产品仍是核心支撑，能源由拖累转为拉动。 9 月，进
口增长 31.0%，较 8 月提高 2.8 个百分点。结构上看，进口高增仍主要集
中于机电产业链，能源金额改善更多为体现价格效应。一是，机电产品拉
动继续增强，增量更多来自自动数据处理设备。9 月，机电产品进口增长
52.2%，拉动整体进口 21.6 个百分点。其中，自动数据处理设备增长
296.5%，拉动 8.9 个百分点，较 8 月增加约 2.8 个百分点；集成电路增长
74.7%，拉动 12.8 个百分点，其数量仅增长 1.7%，均价上涨 71.7%。二
是，能源价格支撑增强，带动金额表现改善。原油和天然气进口金额分别
增长 3.3%和 34.0%，均价分别上涨 31.5%和 36.3%，数量仍分别下降
21.5%和 1.7%。原油、成品油和天然气合计拉动进口约 0.8 个百分点，较
8 月拖累约 1.0 个百分点明显改善。三是，金属资源品表现分化，铜矿仍
主要由价格拉动。铜矿进口增长 27.1%，数量下降 4.2%，均价上涨
32.6%；铁矿石进口下降 7.4%，量价均有所回落。四是，农产品温和增
长，数量修复仍有限。农产品进口增长 5.5%，拉动整体进口约 0.5 个百
分点；大豆进口增长 2.8%，数量下降 7.7%，均价上涨 11.4%。 
往后看，出口仍有支撑，结构改善具备延续条件 。一方面，AI 投资及区域
制造业扩张继续支撑电子、设备和中间品需求；S&P Global 于 2026 年
10 月 9 日发布的全球贸易 PMI 分析指出，科技企业因担忧供给瓶颈和价
格上涨而增加缓冲库存，或可以为订单提供额外支撑2，但这部分需求的
持续性仍需观察。另一方面，部分传统商品及对美出口仍有低基数支撑，
贸易促进和摩擦管控有助于巩固订单。10 月 15 日开幕的广交会将为家
具、纺织、箱包、鞋靴和玩具等行业提供供采对接机会；10 月上旬中欧
磋商在汽车贸易等方面形成成果，有利于稳定相关市场预期 。往后看，一

### Page 3

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 3 
方面需关注 AI 产业链投资及相关出口需求的持续性；另一方面需关注传
统商品数量修复能否延续，以及提前交付对后续出货节奏的影响。

风险提示：全球制造业景气及外需超预期回落；全球 AI 投资需求不及预
期；贸易摩擦及出口限制超预期升级；地缘冲突超预期。

投资评级说明 
投资建议的评级标准  评级 说明 
评级标准为报告发布日后的 6 个月内公司股价 （或行业
指数） 相对同期基准指数的相对市场表现。 其中A 股市
场以沪深 300 指数为基准； 新三板市场以三板成指 （针
对协议转让标的） 或三板做市指数 （针对做市转让标的）
为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准。 
股票评级 
买入 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 10%以上 
增持 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 5%~10%之间 
中性 相对同期基准指数涨幅在-5%~+5%之间 
减持 相对同期基准指数跌幅在 5%以上 
行业评级 
看好 相对表现优于市场 
中性 相对表现与市场持平 
看淡 相对表现弱于市场

## Document 137: [373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) Long Trading 구간LG에너지솔루션(373220) Long Trading 구간Long Trading 구간 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) Long Trading 구간LG에너지솔루션(373220) Long Trading 구간Long Trading 구간 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

LG에너지솔루션 (373220)

Company Brief 
이차전지/석유화학 
Analyst 노우호 
wooho.rho@meritz.co.kr 
RA 황현식 
hyeonsig.hwang@meritz.co.kr Long Trading 구간  
3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후: ESS ↑, BEV ↓  
LG에너지솔루션의 3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후 배터리 섹터는 11/3 미국 중간선거
를 전후로 Trading Long 투자의견이 유효하다. 미국 중간선거 이후 특정정당 우세와 
별개로, 미국 Ex-China ESS 공급망 프리미엄은 유효하다. 동사 역시 그간의 ESS 
사업 난제(단위당 수율, 팩 병목)는 점진적 해소될 점이다. 반면 시장의 BEV 반등 기
대감에 동의하지 않는다. EU 프랑스와 독일의 내연기관 퇴출시점 지연 움직임 및 미
국 연비규제 정책 폐지 결정으로 전통 완성차 고객사들향 BEV 배터리 출하 부진은 
여전하겠다. 미국의 BEV 정책 변화시점은 28.11월 대선으로 지연될 전망이다.

ESS 모멘텀: 한국 제3차 ESS 중앙계약시장 사업자 선정 모멘텀 
대다수 시장 참여자들은 2026년 연말까지 LG에너지솔루션의 미국 ESS 60Gwh  규
모의 잔여 수주 달성 가시성에 집중한다. 해당 ESS 사업 수주 가이던스 관건은 시기
보다 방향성이다. 단, 당사가 주목하는 연말 모멘텀은 한국 정부향 제3차 ESS 중앙
계약시장 사업자 선정으로 판단한다. 3차 규모는 1.13Gw로 1&2차 계약 규모(합산 
1.13Gw) 대비 확대되었다. 또한 언론보도상 LG에너지솔루션은 해당 수주를 위한 핵
심 4대 소재 등 전 Value-chain 국산화를 선언한 바 있다. 해당 수주 확정시, 동사
를 비롯한 해당 Value-chain에 긍정적 모멘텀이 예상된다.

투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 51만원 제시   
26년 연중 지정학 Risk, 에너지 대전환 영향 등에 전력 인프라 수요는 꾸준할 전망
이다. 향후 국내 이차전지 산업은 ‘L’ Shaped Cycle 전망, 반면 동사는 스마트카 T사
향 원통형 전지 출하량 증가, ESS 제조업 이상으로 변모하는 과정으로 산업 평균 성
장률 Outperform 가능하다. 투자의견 Buy 유지, 적정주가는 추정치 상향에 51만원
을 제시한다.

Meritz Research  2026. 10. 15

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 510,000원

## Document 156: AI融资成本攀升：Anthropic拟向黑石提供超10%收益率

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI融资成本攀升：Anthropic拟向黑石提供超10%收益率.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-17
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Anthropic 正推进代号 “Project North Star” 的 600 亿美元芯⽚债务融资，创历史纪录。为筹
得资⾦，公司向锚定投资⼈⿊⽯集团提供逾 10% 收益率，借贷成本仅四个⽉即显着飙升。
此举意在上市前锁定算⼒储备，以⾼息套利押注上市后通过评级提升降低成本。
摘要：
Anthropic 正以远⾼于市场基准的融资成本推进其史上最⼤规模芯⽚融资，折射出 AI 基础设施军
备竞赛背后⽇益⾼企的资本代价。
据英国《⾦融时报》 17 ⽇报道，Anthropic 正向⿊⽯集团（ Blackstone ）提供逾 10% 的收益
率，以换取后者参与这笔代号 "Project North Star" 、总规模⾼达 600 亿美元的债务融资。知情
⼈⼠透露，⿊⽯旗下基⾦将认购其中 90 亿美元，约占 180 亿美元次级贷款的⼀半，并获得 10% 的
票息，加计新发折价及其他费⽤后实际总回报更⾼。
这笔融资规模预计将创下芯⽚融资历史纪录，并将成为衡量投资者对 AI 债务胃⼝的重要⻛向标。

值得关注的是，仅仅四个⽉前， Anthropic 以 8.5% 利率完成代号 "Project Big Sky" 的约 44 亿美元
融资，借贷成本的快速攀升，凸显出投资者对 AI 企业密集融资需求的警惕情绪持续升温。
600 亿融资案：规模创纪录，成本同步飙升
"Project North Star" 旨在为 Anthropic 采购约 1.5 吉⽡由⾕歌开发的定制半导体 —— 即张量处理单
元（ TPU ） —— 提供资⾦。
整体债务结构分为两层：约 420 亿美元为优先级债务，由参与芯⽚研发的 Broadcom 提供担保；
其余 180 亿美元为次级贷款， Blackstone 认购其中 90 亿美元，剩余份额将于未来数⽉⾯向更⼴
泛投资者出售，出售利率可能低于⿊⽯所获条款。预计当资⾦于 2027 年提款完毕后，这部分债
务将进⼊机构 144A 债券市场流通。
Blackstone 拒绝置评， Anthropic 未⽴即回应置评请求。
溢价逾 300 个基点，⻛险溢价需求凸显
根据 ICE 美银 BB 级美国⾼收益债券指数，信⽤状况与 Anthropic 相近的借款⼈在公开债券市场通

常⽀付约 7% 的收益率。 Anthropic 此次向⿊⽯提供的 10% 票息，较该基准⾼出逾 300 个基点，若
计⼊折价及费⽤，实际收益差距更⼤。
这⼀溢价反映出两⽅⾯压⼒：其⼀，投资者对科技企业短期内集中释放的庞⼤融资需求愈发审
慎，要求更⾼的⻛险补偿；其⼆，⼤型融资交易通常需向锚定投资者和早期承诺⽅提供额外溢
价，以确保交易顺利落地。
借贷成本的快速上⾏，亦令 Anthropic 谋求投资级评级的路径更加复杂。评级机构 KBRA 于今年 6
⽉将 Anthropic 评定为投资级以下⼀档，本次次级债务预计将获得类似评级。
IPO 前的战略布局：⾼成本融资换⻓期降费空间
尽管融资成本⾼企， Anthropic 仍选择主动承压，背后存在明确的战略逻辑。据媒体援引知情⼈
⼠透露， Blackstone 已向其投资伙伴表⽰，此次交易是在⾼收益⽔平下投资 Anthropic 关联债务

这⼀叙事本质上是⼀笔时间套利： Anthropic 在上市前以较⾼利率换取必要的算⼒储备，并押注

## Document 167: Anthropic发布内部就业报告：智能体工时达到人类6倍，“元工作时代”来了

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic发布内部就业报告：智能体工时达到人类6倍，“元工作时代”来了.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-18
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

初级技术岗位的招聘占⽐显著下降； AI 智能体的⼯作时⻓已达到⼈类操作者的 6 倍；从直接
执⾏任务，到构建⼯具、监控⼯作流程和管理智能体，员⼯的⼯作重⼼逐渐转移；⼈类注
意⼒成为新的瓶颈，招聘标准也开始从领域专⻓转向⾃主性和判断⼒。
摘要：
美国当地时间 10 ⽉ 16 ⽇， Anthropic 发布了⼀份关于⾃⾝劳动⼒结构的内部研究报告 ——
《 Anthropic 就业之变》（ How Employment is Changing at Anthropic ）。
这项研究基于 Anthropic 内部的就业记录、 AI 使⽤遥测数据、员⼯产出指标、员⼯调查、内部沟
通记录、⽇历数据和招聘决策评分卡，并结合 17 次员⼯深度访谈，系统分析了公司在深度应⽤
AI 后，⼯作内容、招聘结构和劳动⼒需求的变化。
报告提出了四项主要发现：
⾸先， Anthropic 初级技术岗位的招聘占⽐显著下降，从 2025 年初的 27% 降⾄ 2026 年的 9% 。但
公司整体就业规模仍在扩张，受到挤压的是⼊⻔级岗位，⽽⾮岗位总量。

其次， AI 智能体的⼯作时⻓迅速增⻓。截⾄ 2026 年 8 ⽉， Anthropic 员⼯直接指导的交互式智能
体，其⼯作时⻓已达到⼈类操作者的 6 倍。多个岗位的⽣产率有所提升，研究团队通过对照分析
进⼀步评估了 AI 的独⽴贡献。
第三，员⼯的⼯作内容正在发⽣结构性变化。从直接执⾏任务，到构建⼯具、监控⼯作流程和
管理智能体，员⼯的⼯作重⼼逐渐转移。与此同时，⼈类注意⼒成为新的瓶颈，招聘标准也开
始从领域专⻓转向⾃主性和判断⼒。
第四， Anthropic 已开始建⽴早期预警系统和内部⼈员调配机制。但报告指出，企业层⾯的应对
措施仍不⾜以解决⼊⻔岗位收窄等更⼴泛的社会问题。
作为 AI 应⽤最前沿的企业之⼀， Anthropic 内部的这些变化，为观察 AI 可能如何影响其他企业的
⼯作⽅式和招聘需求提供了⼀个样本。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

初级岗位招聘收缩
报告中最引⼈关注的数据，是初级技术岗位招聘⽐例的⼤幅下降。
⾃ 2025 年初以来， Anthropic 技术部⻔中初级员⼯的占⽐从 21% 降⾄ 14% ，初级技术岗位的招聘
占⽐则从 27% 降⾄ 9% 。 2025 年 12 ⽉之后，初级岗位招聘启事的数量出现了尤为明显的下降。
Anthropic ⼊⻔级技术岗位招聘下降
这⼀变化并⾮源于公司收缩。相反， Anthropic 在过去⼀年中规模扩⼤⾄原来的三倍，技术岗位
的绝对数量仍在增⻓。真正发⽣变化的是招聘结构：新增岗位更多集中在中⾼级和专家层级，
初级岗位的⼊⼝则明显收窄。
对初级岗位需求的讨论最早出现在 2026 年 1 ⽉的核⼼技术部⻔。
⼀些重度使⽤ Claude 的团队发现，基础代码编写、漏洞排查等常规⼯程任务越来越多地可以交
由模型完成。性能⼯程师岗位受到的影响尤为明显。⼀位受访者表⽰，这⼏乎在⼀夜之间改变
了团队对⼈才的需求。
Claude 使⽤复杂度较⾼的岗位，初级员⼯增⻓相对较慢
到 2026 年 4 ⽉，⼯程和研究团队已开始系统性地下调低职级岗位的招聘权重。当⽉的内部调查显

⽰，约三分之⼀的机器学习经理和五分之⼀的软件⼯程经理表⽰，他们因使⽤ AI ⽽减少了招聘⼈
数。

### Page 3

初级岗位⾸先受到冲击，与这类⼯作的特点有关。
⼊⻔级⼯作通常具有规则清晰、执⾏性强的特征，⽽这正是当前⼤语⾔模型能够较好处理的领
域。代码编写、数据处理、⽂档整理和客⼾⽀持等任务，都在不同程度上受到 AI 的影响，模型完
成这些⼯作的能⼒也在持续提升。
当模型能⼒每⼏个⽉跃上⼀个台阶，最先被替代的可能会是技能阶梯的 “ 最下端 “ 。
⾮技术部⻔也呈现出类似趋势。通常，企业规模扩张时，⾏政、财务、⼈事等⽀持部⻔也会相
应增加⼈员。但在 Anthropic ，智能体助⼿、⾃动化审核和⼯单服务外包等⽅式，使公司在快速
扩张的同时，仍维持着精简的⽂职⼈员配置。
⼀位⾏政助理开发了⽤于⽇程管理和邮件分类的个⼈助理智能体，并表⽰，团队已经过了利⽤
Claude 增强⼊⻔级⾏政岗位的阶段，⼯作重⼼开始转向更⾼层级的协调与规划任务。
智能体⼯时达到员⼯ 6 倍
招聘结构的变化背后，是智能体在公司内部的快速普及。
2026 年 3 ⽉， Anthropic 内部⾃主运⾏和由员⼯直接指导的两类 AI 智能体，其累计⼯作时⻓⾸次

超过了⼈类员⼯的⼯作时⻓。此后，智能体的⼯作时⻓继续增⻓。到今年 8 ⽉，仅员⼯直接指导
的交互式智能体，其⼯作时⻓就已达到⼈类员⼯的 6 倍。
智能体⼯作时⻓已超过⼈类时⻓
⼤量智能体被部署在软件⼯程领域，承担测试⽤例⽣成、代码检查、基础设施维护和代码库迁
移等任务。这⼀数据尚未包括完全⾃主运⾏的智能体集群。对单个⼯程师⽽⾔，这意味着其可
以同时安排多个智能体持续执⾏任务，并对其⽣成的代码和分析结果进⾏检查。⼯程师的⼯作
也逐渐从亲⾃编写代码转向任务分配和结果审核。
⽣产率的变化也反映在具体数据中。报告显⽰，⼯程师⼈均代码提交量在 6 个⽉内增⻓⾄原来的
5 倍，数据科学家的查询处理量⼤幅上升，律师、应付账款和销售等⾮技术岗位的产出也有所增
⻓。

### Page 4

Anthropic 多个职能部⻔采⽤ AI 技术后，员⼯⽣产率激增
为了排除业务增⻓等因素的⼲扰，研究团队采⽤了对照分析⽅法。他们追踪了 192 名采⽤编码智
能体的⼯程师，并以同期尚未采⽤该⼯具的 74 名⼯程师作为对照组，利⽤个体和⽉份双向固定
效应进⾏估计。
结果显⽰，采⽤编码智能体后，⼯程师的代码提交量出现了统计上显著的快速增⻓。
Anthropic ⼯程师采⽤编码智能体后产出上升， AI ⽣成代码的回退率与⼿写代码⽆显著差异
对于 AI 是否会⽣成低质量代码的问题，报告也进⾏了分析。
研究团队⽐较了同⼀名员⼯在同⼀个⽉、同⼀个代码⽬录下，由智能体直接⽣成和由员⼯⼿动
编写的代码，并以代码在 90 天内被删除或回退的⽐例作为质量评估指标。结果显⽰，两者在代
码质量上不存在统计上显著的差异。⾄少从代码回退率来看， AI ⽣成的代码与⼈类⼿写代码表现
相当。

### Page 5

⾮技术岗位的⽣产率也有所提升。报告显⽰，销售代表采⽤智能体后，完成的客⼾交易数量有
所增加。⼀位客⼾主管估计， Claude ⾃动化了约 20% 的常规⼯作，包括起草邮件和操作客⼾管
理系统。
另⼀位受访者表⽰，⾃动化释放的时间使其能够增加 30% 的客⼾⾯谈量。
从执⾏者到智能体管理者
智能体数量增⻓和⽣产率提升，并没有让员⼯从⼯作中解脱出来。相反，⼈类注意⼒正逐渐成
为智能体产出的瓶颈。
报告显⽰，在交互式会话中，智能体约 80% 的时间处于等待状态，需要⼈类下达指令、确认结
果或提供下⼀步⽅向。
研究团队还按照普通⼈的阅读速度，将智能体输出的 token 换算为阅读时间。结果显⽰，⾮技术
部⻔的交互式 Claude 会话产⽣的内容，阅读起来已⾜以占据员⼯每周相当⼤⼀部分⼯作时间。
仅阅读和处理 AI ⽣成的内容，就成为⼀项不容忽视的⼯作负担。
仅阅读 Claude 的输出，就相当于⼀份全职⼯作量
⾯对这⼀变化，员⼯逐渐形成了⼀种新的⼯作⽅式。报告将其称为 “ 元⼯作 ” （ meta-work ），即围

绕智能体开展的管理性⼯作，包括构建管理⼯具、编写常设指令、设计监控仪表盘，以及安排
不同智能体相互审查。这类⼯作的核⼼，是不再直接执⾏具体任务，⽽是设计和管理由 AI 完成任
务的流程。
相关数据也记录了这⼀转变。 2025 年秋季， “ 元⼯作 ” 约占员⼯交互式 Claude 会话的 10% 。到
2026 年春季，这⼀⽐例升⾄ 22% 。增⻓主要来⾃智能体管理活动，包括为多智能体系统编写规
格说明、安排智能体交叉审查，以及监控⼯作流程的运⾏状态等。

### Page 6

Anthropic 员⼯越来越多地在管理智能体，⽽⾮做实际⼯作本⾝
⼯作⽅式的变化也体现在招聘标准上。
研究团队对 2024 年以来 Anthropic 数千名员⼯的⾯试评分卡进⾏了⾃动编码分析，发现技术岗位
中提及 AI ⼯具使⽤能⼒的评估⽐例从约 5% 升⾄约 15% ，⾮技术岗位则从约 7% 升⾄约 28% 。
与此同时，招聘评估的重点也发⽣了变化。技术岗位中，⾃主性和判断⼒成为增⻓较快的评估
维度，领域专业知识的权重相对下降；⾮技术岗位则更加重视客⼾意识和使命导向，对执⾏技
能的讨论有所减少。⼀位招聘经理表⽰，他预计模型能⼒在不同领域仍会存在差异，因此希望
团队成员能够在不同领域之间灵活切换。
报告中的⼀个案例也体现了这种变化。
Anthropic ⼀⽀负责撰写 Claude.ai 产品问候语的设计团队，在 2026 年春季开发了上下⽂图谱系
统，引导智能体⽣成产品⽂案，从⽽扩⼤了团队的产出能⼒。随后，该团队转向智能体提⽰⼯
程，开始负责撰写模型评估内容和提⽰词。
报告对此作了⼀个概括：曾经通过语⾔影响⼈类⾏为的写作者，如今开始通过语⾔影响 Claude

的⾏为。换⾔之，岗位的名称和外壳没有改变，但其⼯作的内核可能已经不同。
⼊⻔阶梯⾯临挑战
Anthropic 的案例呈现了 AI 对就业结构的影响。作为 AI 应⽤最前沿的企业之⼀，公司内部发⽣的
变化，为观察其他⾏业未来可能⾯临的变化提供了参考。
从⽬前的情况看， AI 对就业的影响⾸先体现在认知劳动的⼊⻔层级。初级程序员、⼊⻔分析师、
⼀线客服、基础⽂案和初级财务等岗位，过去通常是⼤学毕业⽣进⼊职场的起点，如今正逐渐
受到 AI 的影响。
这⼀变化带来的问题不仅是岗位数量的变化。初级岗位也是员⼯积累经验、学习业务流程和逐
步成⻓的重要起点。当这⼀层级收缩，年轻⼈获得第⼀份⼯作和积累职业经验的机会也可能随
之减少。如果企业对新⼈的要求逐渐转向 AI ⼯具使⽤能⼒、判断⼒和⾃主性，那么这些能⼒应该
在什么阶段、通过什么⽅式获得，也成为需要⾯对的问题。
报告讨论了企业可以采取的⼏项措施：

### Page 7

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
237 收藏 分享到 :
写评论
图⽚
第⼀，通过⼴泛的股权共享机制，让员⼯分享 AI 推动⽣产率提升所带来的收益。当员⼯需要管理
智能体、承担更多决策责任时，将个⼈利益与组织⽬标相结合就显得更加重要。
第⼆，建⽴早期预警系统，持续监测 AI 对不同岗位的影响，并提前规划受影响员⼯的内部转岗。
第三，强化内部流动机制。当原有任务逐渐实现⾃动化时，帮助员⼯转向新的⼯作领域，⽽不
是直接将其推向外部劳动⼒市场。
但企业内部的措施仍不⾜以解决更⼴泛的社会问题。 Anthropic 作为⼀家前沿 AI 公司，有条件为
员⼯提供内部转岗和利益共享机制。对于⼤多数传统⾏业的企业和员⼯⽽⾔，这些资源和制度
安排未必具备。谁来帮助这些企业识别岗位变化？谁来承担员⼯技能转换的成本？这些问题已
经超出了单⼀企业的应对范围。

报告末尾有⼀句话值得注意： “ ⼀⽀焦虑担忧的劳动⼒，不是⼀⽀尽⼒⼯作的劳动⼒。 ”
这句话提⽰我们， AI 对就业的影响不仅是经济性的，也是⼼理性和社会性的。
当⼯作的定义在快速变化，当⼊⻔的⻔槛在持续抬⾼，当每个⼈都需要不断证明⾃⼰不会被 AI 替
代，这种持续的不确定性本⾝就会成为⼀种⼴泛的社会⼼理负担。
Anthropic 的实验仍在继续。智能体的能⼒不断增强，应⽤数量持续增⻓，⼈类与 AI 之间的⼯作
分⼯也在不断变化。这场变⾰的最终形态尚不明朗，但有⼀点可以确定：⼯作可能不会消失，
但⼯作的含义、⻔槛和价值排序，正在被改写。
本⽂来源：腾讯科技

Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -2.62 điểm tương ứng -0.15%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Dệt may, Công nghệ và Máy móc là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất trong 
tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -13,987 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã HDB, PNJ và VJC; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng ở mức 
-30 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều mua ròng 1,267 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua nhưng đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -2,363 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 3,726 hợp 
đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -2,702 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam trải qua một tuần đi ngang giằng co, giảm nhẹ và đóng cửa ở 1.735 điểm 
với thanh khoản ở mức thấp, và nhà đầu tư nước ngoài bán ròng cả 5 phiên trong tuần. 
Thông tin vĩ mô tích cực với tăng trưởng GDP quý 3 đạt mức gần 10% là điểm sáng trong 
tuần qua, tuy đã không đủ nâng đỡ thị trường chung. Thay vào đó, tâm lý thị trường thận 
trọng trước số liệu lạm phát ở mức cao, đợt tăng giá xăng dầu trong nước và mặt bằng lãi 
suất trong nước chưa có dấu hiệu hạ nhiệt. Diễn biến tăng của giá dầu thế giới, và lợi suất 
trái phiếu Chính phủ Mỹ cũng là các yếu tố gây áp lực lên thị trường chung. Tâm lý thận 
trọng còn thể hiện qua áp lực bán giải chấp ở một số cổ phiếu riêng lẻ như PNJ, SHB..., dù 
một vài nhóm trụ như dầu khí, tiêu dùng có thời điểm đóng vai trò nâng đỡ chỉ số.

Trần Đức Anh 
Giám đốc Kinh tế Vĩ mô & Chiến lược thị trường 
anhtd@kbsec.com.vn 
07/10/2026

Triển vọng TTCK 4Q2026 
Tích lũy trong vùng nhiễu động

Chúng tôi dự báo vùng điểm hợp lý của chỉ số VNIndex thời điểm cuối năm 2026 
xuống 1,800 đi ểm, giảm mạnh từ mức 1,950 đi ểm đưa ra trong báo cáo gần nhất. 
Vùng điểm này tương ứng:

- Tăng trưởng EPS 2026 so với cùng kỳ của thị trường chung ở mức 33% 
(tăng mạnh so với mức 25% của báo cáo gần nhất), chủ yếu nhờ kỳ vọng 
vào các chính sách thúc đẩy kinh tế của Chính phủ, và lợi nhuận tăng 
trưởng đột biến ở VIC (xem thêm phần Triển vọng hoạt động kinh doanh).

- Mức P/E hợp lý của chỉ số VNIndex thời điểm cuối năm ở mức 13.2 (giảm 
nhẹ so với mức 13.5 thời điểm hiện tại - phản ánh quan điểm các yếu tố 
rủi ro sẽ tiếp tục duy trì). Đây là mức định giá thấp so với bình quân 5 năm 
gần nhất ở 15.1. Nếu loại bỏ yếu tố đột biến ở nhóm Vingroup, chỉ số P/E 
điều chỉnh hiện đã rơi xuống 10.9, thấp hơn các đáy gần nhất năm 2020, 
2022 và 2025. Trong bối cảnh kinh tế Việt Nam duy trì tốc độ tăng trưởng 
cao, lợi nhuận các doanh nghiệp niêm yết liên tục bứt phá, vùng định giá 
này mở ra cơ hội đầu tư trung/dài hạn. Chúng tôi cho rằng ngay khi các rủi 
ro vĩ mô quốc tế (xoay quanh vấn đề Iran), và trong nước (xoay quanh vấn 
đề lãi suất) hạ nhiệt, thị trường chung sẽ có cơ hội tái định giá lên mức cao 
hơn đáng kể so với thời điểm hiện tại. Dù vậy, khác với báo cáo gần nhất 
vào tháng 7, ở báo cáo này, chúng tôi đẩy lui thời điểm các yếu tố rủi ro vĩ 
mô hạ nhiệt sang năm 2027, đồng nghĩa với quan điểm thận trọng hơn về 
thị trường cho quý 4 2026.

Cụ thể, đối với vấn đề lãi suất, áp lực lạm phát lớn dần, đi kèm nhu cầu vốn cao phục 
vụ hoạt động đầu tư cơ sở hạ tầng sẽ khiến mặt bằng lãi suất duy trì ở mức cao cho 
phần còn lại của năm 2026 (dù thanh khoản hệ thống ghi nhận sự cải thiện đáng kể 
trong 1 quý gần đây). Đối với vấn đề xung đột Trung Đông, các cuộc đàm phán vẫn 
đang bế tắc và ít có kh ả năng dòng ch ảy dầu mỏ tại khu vực này được nối lại ngay 
trong năm nay.

Chủ đề đầu tư chúng tôi l ựa chọn cho 4Q2026 là đ ầu tư công, chính sách ti ền tệ hỗ 
trợ, và xung đột Iran.

Xem đầy đủ tại đây.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX hình thành mẫu nến Doji trên khung đồ thị tuần sau hai tuần giảm điểm liên tiếp, 
cho thấy áp lực bán đã phần nào được kiểm soát. Trên khung ngày, nỗ lực hồi phục đầu 
tuần gặp lực cản tại đường SMA50 khiến VNINDEX quay đầu giảm điểm, tiếp tục chớm phá 
đáy ngắn hạn; tuy nhiên vùng hỗ trợ gần đã tạo hiệu ứng thu hút lực cầu bắt đáy, giúp chỉ 
số thu hẹp đà giảm trong phiên cuối tuần. Sau nhịp điều chỉnh khá tiêu cực từ vùng đỉnh 
đầu tháng 9, VNINDEX đang bước vào giai đoạn tìm kiếm điểm cân bằng trở lại và diễn biến 
giằng co điều chỉnh có thể còn kéo dài thêm trong các phiên kế tiếp. Tuy nhiên khi xu 
hướng tăng ngắn hạn tính từ vùng đáy cuối tháng 7 vẫn đang được bảo lưu, chỉ số được kỳ 
vọng sẽ có cơ hội hồi phục trở lại tại điểm đỡ gần quanh 1710 (đây cũng là chốt chặn cần 
giữ để bảo vệ được xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn).

## Document 189: Capital Market Outlook (2026-10-05)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Capital Market Outlook (2026-10-05).pdf
Report source: Merrill Lynch (CMO)
Date: 2026-10-05
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Stronger Growth, Costlier Money
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
The latest GDP report and annual revisions show a sturdier U.S. economy than previously
reported. According to the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA), real GDP grew at a 2.2%
annualized pace in Q2 compared to 1.5% previously estimated, and Q1 was revised
from 2.1% to 2.5%. Both 2024 and 2025 were also revised slightly higher. Most of the

first half of the year revision was in categories related to the Artificial Intelligence (AI)
buildout, but real consumer spending has also been revised slightly higher, accelerating
from 0.7% annualized growth in Q1 to 3.8% in Q2 despite surging energy prices and
slow hiring.
Spending in July and August remained much stronger than labor market conditions alone
would suggest, also averaging 3.7% annualized growth. Upside revisions to net interest

income and personal saving suggest households have benefited from more income than
was previously apparent, with personal saving revised higher back to 2023. The new data,
according to the BEA, show the January to July 2026 savings rate averaged 4.8% versus
3.3% prerevision, strengthening consumer spending underpinnings despite moderate
hiring, high energy prices and rising borrowing costs.
The BEA update also included inflation methodological changes related to categories

inflated by the AI boom—such as portfolio management fees and computer software and
accessories. These improved the inflation picture to some extent—August “core” PCE
inflation was reduced by 0.3 ppts to 3% YoY, bringing it closer to “core” consumer price
index inflation, which was 2.5% YoY and 2% on a three-month annualized basis. While still
above the 2% Federal Reserve (Fed) target, “core” PCE now appears stable at 3% year over
year since January rather than accelerating. These methodological changes are expected

to dampen pass-through of AI-related price pressures into “core” inflation and effects of
stock-market swings on inflation.
They also produced an encouraging improvement in recent PCE inflation momentum.
Three-month annualized “core” PCE inflation slowed from 4.3% at the peak of tariff
effects in February to 2.1% in August. For durable goods, this rate was down from 5.8%
in February to 1.5% in August, and, for services ex-energy and healthcare, it slowed from

4.2% to 2.2%, for example. Inflation is volatile on a three-month change basis, so it
remains to be seen whether this moderation continues without further Fed tightening.
For now, the trends in the San Francisco Fed’s cyclical versus acyclical inflation measures
diverged again in August. As expected, given softening wage growth, prices most sensitive
to the labor market have eased further. At 3% YoY, this inflation category is down from
8% in early 2023 and 4.6% two years ago is back to prepandemic levels and is likely

to ease more (Exhibit 1A). In contrast, supply bottlenecks, high energy prices and other
industry-specific factors have kept acyclical inflation on an uptrend. As shown in Exhibit
1B, the relentless increase in freight costs could prolong upside pressure on this category.
The diesel market has tightened in recent months, boosting its price by 70% YoY,
consistent with Brent oil at around $200 per barrel and large reverberations across
a wide swath of goods and services prices. A government-policy-related decline in

available truck drivers is also raising transportation costs more than expected. So are
tight ocean shipping capacity and longer/more expensive voyages due to the U.S./Iran
War. Supply-chain stress also increased as companies rushed to front-run potential new
tariff increases.
On the positive side for the inflation outlook, unit labor costs (ULC) are up modestly
as robust productivity growth has absorbed much of the wage growth, limiting a major
source of domestic inflation. Upside productivity growth revisions are likely to further

reduce ULC, which could help shift focus from energy and AI-related inflation to
eventual disinflationary effects once oil and refined fuel prices start to ease. Sustained
productivity gains would support growth and margins while limiting inflation and the
need for much higher interest rates.
Investment Implications
We favor selective Equity exposure
to companies with durable demand
and pricing power, while remaining
cautious about businesses most

exposed to fuel, freight, and
financing costs. We would also
gradually increase long-duration
exposure.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Surging nominal growth has boosted corporate revenues (up 13% YoY in Q2, according to the
BEA) while contained unit labor costs helped raise margins and profits more than expected.
With labor costs declining as a share of revenues, investment-related tax incentives and
rock-bottom net interest expense, pre-tax nonfinancial corporate profits as measured in GDP
accounts rose 21% YoY in Q2. Total corporate profits after-tax were up 18.2% YoY, according to

the BEA.
Surging AI-related imports and investment are keeping the business and profits cycle on
an uptrend both here and abroad. According to BofA Global Research, the global economic
cycle backdrop improved further in September, as reflected in a broad-based increase in its
Global Wave1 indicator. Broadening U.S. manufacturing strength, fueled by ongoing AI-driven
investment, a healthier orders backlog and low inventories suggest that the expansion has

room to run—a constructive environment for earnings. Typically, a Global Wave upswing is
consistent with rising earnings revisions ratios and equity price uptrends. According to the
Global Wave, global earnings growth expectations remain exceptionally strong.
Still, absent a resolution of war-related energy and transportation disruptions, the same
industrial strength intensifies inflation pressures. A prolonged supply shock raises the risk of
restrictive interest rates and weakening economic growth. In this context, the surge in the

10-year Treasury yield above 5% is starting to reflect more than optimism about U.S. growth
and strengthening productivity outlook. Oil’s tightest recent correlation with 10-year Treasury
yields in 35 years suggests a common energy-price thread behind rising yields in developed
countries.
Bond market spillovers from normalizing Japan yields and capital repatriation are another
common thread. Higher yields on foreign sovereign bonds such as in Japan—which has

escaped stagnation and deflation by intensely pursuing investment-led growth—weaken the
relative appeal of Treasurys. That distinction matters for portfolios. A yield increase driven
mainly by durable productivity gains would come with a stronger outlook for future income
and earnings. A yield increase driven by cost shocks and greater uncertainty raises financing
costs without the same benefit.
In sum, the economy remains more resilient than expected, but the bond market is also

charging more for an uncertain inflation outlook given untethered government debt and
cooling global demand for Treasurys spurred by geopolitical tensions and Japan capital
repatriation. The case for much further tightening becomes stronger if wage growth
strengthens, productivity lags, and unit labor costs reaccelerate, causing cyclical inflation to
also pick up steam. The case becomes weaker if that measure stays contained while fuel and
freight pressures recede or rising Treasury yields reduce asset prices and economic growth.

MARKET VIEW
Yields, Yen and Iran: What Haunts the “House”
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
We recently highlighted the three-front challenge facing Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent:
engineering a stronger yen, containing the 10-year Treasury yield and securing a diplomatic
resolution to the conflict with Iran.

Bessent recently has delivered one of the year’s more memorable lines while discussing
Treasury policy and the yen: “I have asymmetric information. I am the house now.” 2 Yet several
challenges remain: the 10-year Treasury yield is near levels last seen in the early 2000s, the
yen remains structurally weaker, and a phased agreement to reopen the Strait of Hormuz
appears remote. The resulting strain on energy markets keeps inflation risks elevated.

Put differently, oil prices, Treasury yields and the dollar-yen exchange rate are all trending
against Bessent’s objectives. Below, we examine how each variable is haunting his “house.”
Haunting the “House” is the Yen. Since the U.S. and Japan mounted a record $98.7B
coordinated intervention in late July after the yen weakened to a 40-year low near 164 per
dollar, the currency has appreciated roughly 4% against the dollar. The move was further
supported on September 18 when the BoJ raised rates by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%,

their highest level since the mid-1990s (Exhibit 2A). However, the split 7–2 vote and Governor
Ueda’s limited forward guidance leave the path of further tightening uncertain. The key
question is whether additional BoJ hikes can produce sustained appreciation in the yen, or
merely reinforce the temporary relief provided by the summer intervention.
Bessent, meanwhile, has repeatedly signaled his preference for a stronger yen. Partly because
yen stability is also a U.S. financing concern. Persistent currency weakness could prompt

Japan to support its currency by selling foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys—which would
be an unwelcome prospect as Washington partially relies on foreign capital flows to fund its
large fiscal deficits. That potential “haunts” the Treasury Secretary.  At precisely the moment
Washington needs foreign capital to finance its enormous fiscal deficits, it would be inopportune
for one of America’s largest creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities.

Haunting the “House” are Yields. The surge in yields runs counter to a White House intent
on lowering borrowing costs. The benchmark 10-year yield has broken above the 5% threshold
and climbed to levels not seen since 2007. At around 5.30%, it stands more than a full
percentage point above where it began the year (Exhibit 2B). The level is striking but so is the
speed of the move: Stronger-than-expected economic data, including a survey showing U.S.

5 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
business activity expanded at the fastest pace in more than five years, helped trigger an 18
bps surge in the 10-year yield—among its largest daily moves in decades. The 5-year yield also
topped 5% for the first time since 2007, while the 30-year yield reached 5.63%, its highest
level since 2002.
Compounding the move is inflation, running above the Fed’s 2% target for 67 months,

large fiscal deficits and the prospect of continued growth in Treasury issuance. Against this
backdrop, Treasury bought back $4.1 billion of long-dated debt, versus a maximum of $6
billion. That effort has done little to arrest the selloff. A subsequent auction priced nearly $4
billion of 7-year notes at 5.085%, the highest yield for that maturity in 33 years.
All in, the MOVE index, a key gauge of Treasury market volatility, jumped from 78 to 95 on

Wednesday September 23 and then to 104 the following day, its highest level since the early
days of the Iran war (Exhibit 3A).
Haunting the “House” is the Iran conflict. Early expectations that military pressure and
economic sanctions would quickly bring Iran to the negotiating table have yet to materialize.
Iranian officials have signaled that a full reopening of the Strait of Hormuz remains unlikely
while sanctions stay in place. Although talks between Washington and Tehran are continuing,

conflicting signals and lingering skepticism from both sides have kept tensions elevated.
As a result, oil prices have remained elevated in a range even as some Middle East supply
disruptions began to ease.
Startling is that Brent crude is up more than 60% this year, but refined product prices
have risen even faster. Ongoing disruptions to energy flows through the Strait of Hormuz,
combined with Ukrainian strikes on Russian refining infrastructure have tightened global

supplies of crude oil and refined fuels. Saudi Arabia has restored some drone-disrupted flows,
providing roughly 3.5 million barrels a day of relief (roughly 3% of global consumption).
The squeeze is mostly acute in diesel markets, where inventories remain constrained and
prices continue to climb (Exhibit 3B). U.S. diesel prices recently surpassed $6.50 per gallon,
the highest level on record, just as Russia has extended restrictions on diesel exports into

October. Against this backdrop, proposals to ban U.S. fuel exports risks worsening the
imbalance. According to S&P Global, an export ban could reduce refinery runs by nearly 2
million barrels per day and significantly cut gasoline production, potentially driving gasoline
and jet fuel prices even higher. As long as tensions persist, the oil impulse remains a key
impediment to structurally lower inflation.
Investment implications. Treasury Secretary Bessent increasingly sits at the intersection of

monetary policy, fiscal policy, foreign policy and national security. We remain constructive on
the U.S. economy and overweight U.S. Equities but are watching as these fronts challenge the
narrative. There is a great deal riding on the “house” versus the key macro variables of today.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
When It Comes to Attracting Foreign Capital, the U.S. Remains Exceptional
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
There may be no better vote of confidence in a country than the willingness of
foreigners to put their money there. And by that measurement, the U.S. remains the
exception—both portfolio and FDI inflows are currently running at record highs based

on the latest data. This dynamic is one key reason why we remain constructive on U.S.
assets over the longer term.
Notwithstanding all the handwringing about America’s fiscal deficits, tariffs and rising
government debt, global investors sunk some $942B in net purchases of U.S. Equities in
the 12 months to July, the highest rolling 12-month total on record. In Q2, net purchase
of U.S. Equities soared to $426B, up 62% from the same period a year ago (Exhibit 4A).

By country, flows were down from China—not surprisingly—but strong from Taiwan and
South Korea, who have taken to recycling their massive AI-generated trade surplus back
into the U.S. In the end, strong portfolio inflows suggest that international investors
remain willing to increase their exposure to the world's largest capital market—that’s
bullish for U.S. assets.
Meanwhile, Exhibit 4B highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be

inside the U.S., with FDI inflows totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year.
That is a staggering 103% jump from a year ago and means FDI inflows are on track for
a record year, estimated at $480B for 2026.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Two, within the $32 trillion-plus economy with extraordinarily consumer

purchasing power, foreign firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment
than just trade (exports). Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers,
semiconductors, power generation, etc., has created numerous potential investment
opportunities for many foreign firms that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging
capex cycle. Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly
developed technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.

In the end, double-barreled capital inflows speak to the absolute and relative
attractiveness of the U.S. economy and capital markets. Stay invested in America—the
rest of the world is.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

ICE BofA MOVE Index is a market-implied measure of U.S. bond market volatility.
Consumer Price Index measures the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a market basket of consumer goods and services.
Baltic Dry Index is a daily shipping and trade index published by the Baltic Exchange in London that measures the cost of moving raw materials like iron ore, coal, and grain by sea.
Volatility Index (VIX) is a real-time market indicator that measures the stock market's expected volatility over the next 30 days.

or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

for a changing

GROWTH ON TRACK DESPITE THE TURBULENCE
Growth was stronger than expected in Q2 and is set to strengthen further in the coming quarters. According to our fore-
casts, it will reach 1.5% in 2027 after 1.1% in 2026. This growth is expected to be driven by investment and its knock-on
effect on exports. On an annual average basis, inflation is expected to stand at 3% in 2026 and 2.8% in 2027, peaking at

the end of 2026. The ECB is expected to continue its monetary tightening. At this stage, a single further rate rise, aimed at
keeping inflation expectations anchored, is expected in December. The impact of rising interest rates (key policy rates and
market rates) on public finances and private sector borrowing conditions will be worth monitoring.
GROWTH: BEYOND THE SHOCKS, A RESILIENT MOMENTUM
Growth in the Eurozone remained robust in the first half of 2026, at

0.25% q/q in Q1 and 0.33% q/q in Q2, excluding Ireland (where economic
activity is volatile). It was driven by Germany, where signs of a recovery
are becoming clearer (see Germany). Our baseline scenario forecasts
growth of 1.1% in 2026, followed by 1.5% in 2027 on an annual average
basis. This growth is expected to be driven by the strengthening of
trends already underway in public and private investment (particularly
in defence and AI) and in exports (trade in services and intra-zone trade

in goods). Household consumption is likely to be resilient too. According
to our forecasts, job creation would continue to underpin household
incomes, thereby cushioning the impact of inflation.
A NEW LEASE OF LIFE FOR INDUSTRY
The Eurozone is undergoing one of the most profound industrial trans-
formations in its history. This is giving rise to divergences between key
sectors but, above all, to new drivers of growth whose effects are beco-
ming increasingly apparent (see our analysis). Indeed, the signs of an

economic recovery are growing stronger. Business sentiment indicators
show a marked improvement in the economic outlook across most sec-
tors (Chart 2), driven primarily by electronics (AI, digitalisation), the
automotive sector and aerospace. European industry is thus benefi -
ting from strong demand for capital goods; a structural trend that will
continue to be fuelled by ongoing investment cycles in AI, defence and
electrification. The introduction of minimum European content quotas

in the draft law on the industrial accelerator, together with the Com -
mission’s intention to tighten the criteria for access to European public
procurement markets, will provide additional support for European in-
dustry in the longer term.
FOREIGN TRADE: A REPOSITIONING THAT IS PAYING OFF
Following a decline in 2026, linked to rising energy import costs, the
trade surplus is expected to rise to around 3.0% of GDP in 2027 (of
which 2% of GDP for goods and 1% for services). Growth in European ex-

ports has slowed compared with its pre-Covid trend and is being ham-
pered by the decline in Chinese and US markets. However, Eurozone
exports in volume rose significantly in Q2 (+3.4% q/q).
More importantly, since Liberation Day (April 2025), Eurozone coun -
tries have found significant sources of growth amongst other European
partners, which has enabled them to cushion the decline in exports to
certain third markets. This is evident in exports to the rest of the EU,

EFTA countries (Switzerland in particular) and, to a lesser extent, the
United Kingdom, Ukraine and the Balkans (Chart 3). The momentum of
intra-European trade therefore remains very strong. It is expected to
strengthen further with the anticipated investments in defence, AI and
electrification.
FINANCING: FINANCIAL CONDITIONS ARE EXPECTED TO REMAIN TIGHT
Financial conditions in the Eurozone remained tight in Q2 2026, accor-
ding to indices calculated by the ECB (including the BIG [Broad Inter -

mediation Gauge]). They are expected to tighten more significantly in
the second half of the year: i/ the rise in long-term bond yields, which
has accelerated since the outbreak of the conflict in Iran, is expected to
continue; and ii/ the ECB will continue its monetary tightening (+25 bps

The bank
for a changing
world
in Q3, then in Q4). For the time being, the debt burden is still weighing
only moderately on public finances due to the long average maturity of
countries’ debt (8.2 years on average in the Eurozone in July), but this
burden will increase by 2030.
On the corporate front, the rise in interest rates has remained contained
(+2 bps since January 2026, reaching 3.9% in July), which has contri -

buted to the strong growth in new investment loans (cumulated over
one year, +9.1% y/y in July compared with 15.3% y/y in September 2025).
By contrast, cumulative net debt securities issues over one year re -
mained stable in July, whilst cumulative net issues of listed shares over
one year halved month on month.
Rising interest rates will continue to hamper new variable-rate
home loans to households (around 65% of new lending) more signi -
ficantly than fixed-rate loans, for which rates stood in July at 3.69%

(+4 bps m/m) and 3.36% (+2 bps m/m) respectively. New home loans,
which are stable in 2026 on a cumulative over one year basis, are there-
fore unlikely to see any improvement. A fall in real-estate transactions
could weigh on new consumer credit through a reduction in expenditure
on home furnishings and renovations, whereas consumer credit has
been fairly buoyant so far (year-to-date, +5.2% y/y in July compared
with +1.4% y/y in January).
LABOUR MARKET: RESILIENCE YET TO BE CONFIRMED

Employment in the Eurozone rose again by an average of 0.1% q/q in
the first half of 2026, driven by Spain and Portugal. Given the growth
outlook in these two countries, this momentum is likely to continue,
whilst business sentiment surveys in Germany suggest that employ -
ment is beginning to stabilise. Nevertheless, employment levels are
plateauing (France, the Netherlands) or even falling in other countries
(Austria, Finland), which is likely to slow the decline in the Eurozone

unemployment rate, meaning it is expected to remain close to current
levels in 2027. Furthermore, second-round effects of the rise in inflation
on wages cannot be ruled out: the ECB’s wage tracker suggests wage
growth of close to 3% by summer 2027 (2.9% y/y in June 2027, compared
with 2.6% in September).
INFLATION: A PEAK EXPECTED IN LATE 2026
Changes in wage dynamics will be a key factor in the inflation out -
look for 2027, whilst energy price rises are picking up again, driven

by the conflict in the Middle East. This rise is even sharper than in
the spring for gas (linked to restocking ahead of winter) and is likely
to feed through to electricity prices in several countries. We therefore
anticipate inflation peaking at 3.7% y/y in Q4 2026, before easing as the
energy component falls. On an annual average, headline inflation is
expected to stand at 3% in 2026 and then at 2.8% in 2027.
At this stage, the pass-through of rising energy prices to other com -

ponents of the consumer price index, particularly services, remains li -
mited. The alternative core indicators monitored by the ECB, including
the PCCI (Persistent and Common Component of Inflation, 2.1% in July),
remain close to the 2% target. Core inflation, however, is expected to
peak two months later than headline inflation (3% in March 2027).
Inflationary risks are nevertheless tilted to the upside. These are lin -
ked to food prices, which are expected to be affected by the energy

shock and weather conditions (summer drought, El Niño); they could
also stem from a delayed impact of energy price rises on underlying
inflation. The rise in prices of energy-dependent inputs (notably che -
micals and plastics) and those linked to the AI sector is expected to be
sustained. Growth, which we expect to remain robust over the coming
quarters, will also fuel underlying inflationary pressures.
MONETARY POLICY: TIGHTENING WITHOUT SLOWING (GROWTH)

Following two 25-basis-point rises (in June and September), we ex -
pect a third in December, which would bring the deposit facility rate
to 2.75%. It would then be in slightly restrictive territory (we estimate
the upper bound of the neutral rate to be 2.5%). This rise would be
aimed primarily at anchoring inflation expectations and, consequently,
at curbing the pass-through effects of inflation. Monetary tightening
would therefore remain limited. Indeed, the overshoot of the inflation

target would be temporary, and the operational framework adopted by
the ECB in June 2025 allows it to tolerate this, provided it remains brief.
However, it cannot be ruled out that the ECB might take further action
if the decline in inflation proves slower than expected.
PUBLIC FINANCES ARE CONTINGENT ON RISING INTEREST RATES
The fiscal impulse in the Eurozone is expected to be clearly positive in
2026 due to the rise in defence and infrastructure spending in Germany.

This will contribute to a widening of the country’s primary deficit (from
+0.9 pp of GDP to 2.8%, according to our forecasts). The improvement in
public finances is expected to stall in both France and Italy, at least in
2026, due to the impact of inflation on the debt burden via index-linked
bonds. This trend is expected to reverse in 2027 with less expansionary
fiscal policies. Nevertheless, the increase in the debt service burden
would fuel a further rise in the public deficit, which would thus reach

around 3.3% and 3.5% of GDP in 2026 and 2027 respectively (compared
with 2.9% in 2025). Against this backdrop, the Eurozone’s public debt-
to-GDP ratio is projected to exceed 90% of GDP in 2026 and reach 93%
of GDP in 2027, according to our forecasts.
Article completed on 15 September 2026

## Document 290: Supply chain risks are changing. Shouldn’t financing change too_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Supply chain risks are changing. Shouldn’t financing change too_.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-09
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

As climate risks have a larger impact on supply chains, the
need to ﬁnance resilience-building initiatives is increasing.
Banks have an important role to play by expanding their
ﬁnancing toolkit, while companies with proactive resilience
strategies can gain greater access to capital
Coco Zhang
As climate risks become supply chain risks, ﬁnancing supply chain resilience is
oﬀering opportunities for banks and companies to explore together
Climate and weather-related shocks are a growing source of supply

chain risk. In August, the Rhine River’s water levels hit a record low
since 1880, hindering trade ﬂows across Europe. In 2024, Hurricane
Helene aﬀected global tech and medical supply chains by ﬂooding
key ultra-pure quartz production facilities in the US. The upcoming
“very strong” El Niño could substantially disrupt temperature and
precipitation patterns across the globe next year, putting, for
example, food production and trade at risk.
These events underscore the need to build supply chain resilience.

Extreme weather is now a more impactful part of the supply chain

risk landscape alongside geopolitics and other risks.
Yet signiﬁcant gaps remain. First, while the awareness of supply
chain climate risks is rising, many companies have yet to
acknowledge their ﬁnancial materiality. Second, the uptake of
ﬁnancing solutions has not kept pace with supply chain resilience
needs, despite trade ﬁnance supporting around 90% of global
trade. This leaves ﬁnancial institutions well positioned to expand
ﬁnancing solutions that support corporate supply chain resilience.

Below is how they can do it.
Expanding banks’ toolkit for supply chain
resilience
1. Broaden the use of sustainable ﬁnance
Today, the most common way banks support supply chain
resilience is through sustainable ﬁnance – in other words,
sustainability-labelled debt. However, scaling up this market
remains challenging because supply chain resilience is not a
standalone category under widely used frameworks such as the
International Capital Market Association’s (ICMA’s) Green Bond

Principles and Green Loan Principles. This makes it harder for issuers
to ﬁnance projects under a dedicated supply chain resilience
theme. As a result, there is also limited data on the total volume of
sustainable ﬁnance supporting supply chain resilience. The global
green bond use-of-proceeds allocation data, for example,
demonstrates this challenge.

But at the same time, this creates an opportunity. Supply chains
are closely linked to many existing sustainable ﬁnance themes.
Projects that strengthen supply chain resilience can often be
ﬁnanced under established categories such as climate adaptation
(e.g. resilient infrastructure and information systems), sustainable
water management (e.g. ﬂood mitigation), circular economy, and
so on. This requires banks to better understand which supply chain

challenges matter most to each client and identify the
sustainability solutions best suited to their needs.
2. Look beyond labelled sustainable debt
The opportunity to ﬁnance supply chain resilience extends well
beyond traditional sustainable debt markets. Here are some
examples:
Supply chain resilience ﬁnance
The traditional supply chain and trade ﬁnance solutions
can be tailored to integrate resilience objectives. For
example, guarantees can support cross-border trade in

climate-resilient goods, while preferential ﬁnancing terms
can reward companies that actively manage supply chain
risks. By incorporating supply chain risk assessments into
ﬁnancing programmes and linking working capital
solutions to supplier resilience, these products can help
companies better withstand disruptions and support long-
term growth.
Climate and resilience tech ﬁnance

Banks can provide ﬁnancing and advisory services to help
scale and deploy technologies that strengthen supply
chain resilience. Examples include water management
technologies, regenerative agriculture, and indoor farming.
These solutions can improve the upstream supply chains
of industries such as food, beverage, and hospitality. As
interest in resilience technologies grows, ﬁnancial
institutions are increasingly developing tailored ﬁnancing
solutions to support their adoption.

Blended ﬁnance
Some resilience technologies, projects, and markets may
be too risky for commercial banks to ﬁnance on their own.
Blended ﬁnance oﬀers a compelling solution, as it
combines private capital with support from multilateral
development banks or other public institutions. This can
reduce risk for lenders while helping companies access
more aﬀordable funding for resilience-related investments.
For banks, these solutions reﬂect a shift in mindset and strategy.

Moving beyond ﬁnancing individual transactions, banks now play
an important role in helping build a resilient ecosystem of suppliers,
infrastructure, and logistics networks.

As banks work to expand their ﬁnancing oﬀerings to enhance
supply chain resilience, companies would beneﬁt from doing their

part as well. My colleague Rico Luman has written about some best
practices companies can adopt in their day-to-day operations.
These include building up inventory of high-risk supply materials,
diversifying suppliers and transport routes, and improving supply
chain visibility. Financing solutions that link resilience to working
capital, for example, can help companies strengthen these eﬀorts.
Companies can also look beyond day-to-day operations and invest

in longer-term resilience initiatives, such as climate-resilient
materials, water management technologies, and other adaptation
solutions. These investments can help create more resilient supply
chains while opening up additional ﬁnancing opportunities.
Treat climate risks as business risks – and
ﬁnancing opportunities
As climate risks become supply chain risks, resilience needs
ﬁnancing. The available toolkit for ﬁnancing supply chain resilience

oﬀers more potential than what the market is consistently tapping
today. This creates considerable opportunities for banks and
companies to explore together.
